Continue developing my existing AI trading software into a dependable, fully automated local application with one launch action, a scalable target of 320 independently trading agents supervised by one shared brain service, a shared real-time market feed, exactly three main pages, optional real-money broker connections, and a proactive speaking assistant inspired by JARVIS.

Act as the project's senior software engineer, quantitative research engineer, execution-systems engineer, security engineer, and product designer. Work in the actual repository. Implement functioning software, verify it, fix defects, and deliver a runnable application. A polished mockup, proposal, or collection of disconnected modules is not completion.

REVISION 13 — BOUNDED IMPROVEMENTS AND ACTUAL RESULTS

The user requests continued improvement toward profitable day trading. Improve net economics and operational reliability through registered experiments; do not promise large profits, target a desired win rate by changing definitions, increase leverage to mask a weak edge, or optimize repeatedly until an attractive chart appears. There remain exactly two user-facing document files. Research candidates in this prompt are separate from the qualifying list.

Completed additional research: Batch 02 tested 36 spot configurations with six fixed variants in three matched pairs, and Batch 03 tested six long/short futures configurations with two fixed models. Combined with Batch 01, there are 90 base configurations and 1,224 development/reporting metric rows. These are related configurations, not 90 independent strategies. Extra validation rows, if any, are recorded separately. The 124 specification-card count and 212 overlapping discovery-index count are unchanged.

Current historically qualifying model count across the new batches: 0. Batch 02 positive full-year base configurations: 0/36. Batch 03 positive full-year base configurations: 0/6. Do not represent a positive development result as a qualifying or live-ready strategy. Full results, frozen code and provenance are embedded in the research appendix below.

WHAT CHANGED AND WHY

The first batch’s high turnover and approximately cost-sized average losses motivated fresh-entry signals, a maximum of two entries per UTC day, a four-bar cooldown, and a maximum four-hour intraday holding period at 15-minute resolution. These changes aim to reduce repeated weak entries; they do not create an economic edge by themselves. Do not call lower loss or drawdown evidence of better predictive power when the change simply reduces exposure.

Test entry filters in matched pairs: EMA pullback reclaim with and without a completed-hour trend and range screen; buffered channel breakout with and without that regime, range and relative-volume screen; VWAP reversal with and without the regime and movement screen. Hourly observations become usable only after the entire hour completes. The movement screen compares a volatility or deviation measure with assumed costs; it is not a forecast of profit or a measured liquidity guarantee.

The midnight UTC crypto opening-range adaptation is not prioritized in this round because it lacks a stock-exchange opening rationale. This does not assert that every time-of-day crypto method fails. The separate futures experiment tests symmetric long/short signals on actual perpetual futures, rather than treating free spot shorting as available. Include actual funding-event timestamps and rates, and mark-to-market prices. Official funding-history and mark-price mechanics are referenced in [S98]. A 15-minute funding-price approximation remains an explicit limit.

SELECTION AND VALIDATION RULES

Freeze exact rules, code hashes and thresholds before results. Batch 02 requires a positive median net return, at least four of six positive instruments, positive median results in both 2025 halves, positive median higher-cost and extra-delay results, at least 50 trades per instrument, and no instrument exceeding 10% marked drawdown. Batch 03 uses the same gates with at least two of three positive instruments. Select at most one model from each bounded experiment by full-year median base return; never pick only its winning instruments.

Only a development passer may open January–September 2026 validation. Freeze its selection first; apply identical rules and costs without retuning, and additionally require a positive median in each validation quarter. If no model passes development, retain 2026 as unused validation. These historical protocols are not genuinely prospective trials, and no formal multiple-testing-adjusted confidence or probability of backtest overfitting is claimed. Record all prior related trials when judging future evidence.

Base costs remain 10 bps fee and 2 bps adverse slippage per side; stress uses 10 bps fee and 10 bps slippage per side. Extra-delay tests use base costs and delay signal-driven execution by one additional 15-minute bar. These are assumptions rather than account-specific measured costs. Position sizing stays at at most 10% of current cash/equity-related collateral, capped by 0.1% of prior completed-bar quote volume. No increased leverage is used to create apparent profitability. Stops and targets are evaluated on completed closes and filled at the later open; these are not intrabar stop-order tests.

NEXT RESEARCH REQUIREMENTS

Reject failed configurations as tested. A future experiment must have a distinct economic rationale and registered limits rather than recycled thresholds. Improve historical stock data access and point-in-time universes before claiming any stock result. For decentralized meme coins, obtain historical pool and token state plus failed-token outcomes before adding a qualifying entry. Current centralized meme-coin spot tests do not validate launchpad or on-chain execution.

Before operational eligibility, measure actual authorized-account fees, spread, slippage, order-size capacity, latency and recovery behavior. Futures research additionally needs verified margin tiers, liquidation, collateral and ADL handling; historical candle P&L cannot establish that an actual account could execute every trade. No trading system, fleet, broker connection, or live profit has been created by these research experiments.

REVISION 12 — TWO DOCUMENTS AND AUTOMATIC RESEARCH

DOCUMENT PACKAGE: EXACTLY TWO FILES

The two user-facing document files are AI_Trading_System_Master_Prompt.md and Backtested_Working_Strategies.md. This prompt contains the complete build requirements, research candidate specifications, discovery index, and source register. A separate catalog, report, or ZIP is not required to use these two documents. Internal software modules and research evidence may use their normal project structure; do not mistake this two-document requirement for a restriction on the application’s source files.

Backtested_Working_Strategies.md contains only strategy configurations that actually qualify under the stated evaluation requirements, with exact tested markets, dates, rules, costs, and evidence. The current qualifying count is 0. All 48 configurations from Batch 01 had negative full-year returns under its base fee/slippage assumptions. Do not populate the file with losing configurations, untested hypotheses, published claims that were not independently reproduced, or before-cost winners. The 124 cards and 212 research-index entries in this prompt remain research candidates and must not be advertised as tested winners.

An empty qualifying list is a valid result. Start System may start the brain, agents, market monitoring, and automatic research while trade entries remain blocked by strategy eligibility. Do not enable an unverified fallback, invent a profitable strategy, or relax the evaluation requirements to fill the list. New qualifying additions require versioned reproducible evidence, net economics, an appropriately independent evaluation period, sufficient observations, and documented execution limitations. Historical qualification never guarantees future profits or automatically establishes live eligibility.

The latest user request explicitly authorizes historical backtesting. Keep testing automatic and internal, with no manual backtest button or fourth page. The existing three-page interface, Start System flow, target 320 independent traders, separate brain service, and shared capabilities remain unchanged.

There is no finite, verifiable list of every working strategy on Earth. Keep expanding a deduplicated evidence registry; do not label published, popular, or profitable-in-one-sample methods as universally working. Document negative results and failed replications as carefully as positive results. A strategy count is not an effectiveness metric.

AUTOMATIC RESEARCH PIPELINE

Each research job must carry a source, economic rationale, parent-family ID, precise universe, decision timestamp, inputs available at that timestamp, entry and exit rules, holding limits, sizing, costs, execution assumptions, parameter version, code hash, data hashes, and status. Missing fields block a performance claim. Separate source replication from proposed adaptation and preserve the source horizon. Record all parameter trials; do not rename a failed configuration to erase its history.

Use these states: DISCOVERED, RULES_INCOMPLETE, DATA_BLOCKED, IMPLEMENTED_RESEARCH_ONLY, TEST_COMPLETE, REJECTED_UNDER_TEST, VALIDATION_PENDING, and separately authorized operational states. Historical positive returns alone cannot advance a method into trading. Every configured agent can read every catalog entry; each executes only compatible, eligible versions within its assignment and the shared capital limit.

Use event-time and availability-time independently. Reconstruct historical membership, delistings, splits, dividends, symbol changes, listings, and contract migrations. Reject duplicate or impossible observations; identify gaps and stale quotes. A checksum verifies archive integrity, not the economic correctness of the source. Record provider disagreement when equivalent independent observations exist.

Predeclare parameter choices and evaluation periods. Separate training, model selection, and untouched evaluation where sufficient data exist; purge overlapping labels and use embargoes when required. Report the number and dependence of all trials. Repeatedly reusing a holdout makes it part of development. Sharpe ratios from selected winners require selection-bias treatment; do not claim a deflated Sharpe or overfitting probability unless computed with justified inputs.

Calculate returns after modeled fees, spread, slippage, impact, financing, borrow, funding, gas, and failed transactions as applicable. Report cash and suitable exposure-aware benchmarks, turnover, drawdown, trade counts, tail outcomes, capital usage, and capacity. A fill at the next bar open assumes instantaneous boundary execution; disclose that simplification. Tick or order-book strategies cannot be validated using only OHLC bars. Test session cutoffs, recovery, duplicate-order prevention, and accounting independently of profitability.

STOCK RESEARCH IMPROVEMENTS

Prioritize complete specifications for ST021/ST122, ST022/ST121, stock momentum and reversal, and timestamped event methods. Use exchange calendars including early closes and daylight saving, point-in-time universes, delisted names, corporate actions, and quote-aware fills. Separate regular and extended sessions. Short strategies require historical borrow availability and costs; missing borrow is not free unlimited shorting. Financial-statement and news features use publication availability, not fiscal period end or a later revised database value. No intraday stock backtest is claimed in Batch 01: a suitable historical feed and executable stock rules remain missing.

CRYPTO RESEARCH IMPROVEMENTS

Separate spot direction, perpetual funding, dated basis, venue arbitrage, and liquidity provision. Funding and basis methods require funding timestamps, mark/index series, margin rules, collateral returns, and both executable legs. Spot candles alone cannot test them. Rebuild the tradable universe through time; retain delisted pairs and venue failures. Keep USDT-denominated results distinct from USD results and account for collateral risk when appropriate. Compare session anchoring and hold duration only in a newly registered experiment, without retroactively selecting whichever clock won.

MEME-COIN RESEARCH IMPROVEMENTS

Maintain a discovery universe covering themes, chains, launch stages, and token designs, but record actual dataset coverage. For each token preserve chain ID, contract address, deployment time, pool lineage, migration history, decimal changes, verified price units, and data availability. Reconstruct token permissions and liquidity at the historical decision time. Include failed, abandoned, delisted, and unsellable tokens; do not choose a universe from today’s survivors.

Model route-specific executable output, fees, transfer taxes, reserve or tick liquidity, gas, confirmation delay, reverted transactions, and adverse execution. If no executable exit existed, record the stranded position and recovery assumptions explicitly; never fill at a chart price. Separate tradeable centralized listings from new decentralized launches. Wash trading and related manipulation can distort apparent activity [S93]. Treat manipulation filters as unverified risk controls until their incremental effect is evaluated; they are not additional profitable strategies.

Compare any new filter with a matched baseline using the same historical universe and timestamps. Measure false exclusions, missed opportunities, exit failures, and net results, not just the return of surviving selected tokens. DOGE, SHIB, and PEPE spot candles in Batch 01 provide no validation for every meme category, a launchpad, or an on-chain route.

CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION BOUNDARY

Batch 01 is a standalone research program with no broker connection or order-submission capability. It implements four explicitly defined long-only intraday adaptations linked to ST002, ST003, ST021, and ST024, across six Binance spot pairs in 2025 at 5- and 15-minute intervals. It evaluates five cost assumptions and three reporting periods. These are 48 base configurations and 720 metric rows, not 720 independent strategies. The four new cards ST121–ST124 are research candidates, not additional tested implementations. The concise qualifying result and tested scope are in Backtested_Working_Strategies.md; retain full run evidence internally. No published study is claimed as exactly replicated.

Batch 01 completed: all 48 base-cost configurations had negative full-year returns; 24 were positive before costs. Base costs assume 10 bps fee plus 2 bps adverse slippage per side, not measured account costs. Reject these exact configurations under those assumptions; do not infer that every implementation of their parent families fails. No configuration is promoted. Every other card and research-index entry remains untested in this batch. Maintain a machine-readable coverage manifest distinguishing partial adaptation tests from full-card coverage. Do not mark all 124 cards as tested because four family examples ran. No strategy in this delivery is certified live-ready and no trading fleet is running.

Ask only for missing credentials, account choices, permissions, or decisions that materially block progress. Keep the map current as work proceeds. Deliver complete vertical workflows in runnable increments rather than accumulating disconnected features.

I do not want a backtest button, backtesting page, or manual strategy-testing features. The agents own research, evaluation, parameter selection within approved bounds, monitoring, and readiness decisions automatically in the background. Build exactly three top-level pages:
- AI Trading System.
- AI Supercomputer — Agent Activity.
- Brokers & Accounts.

Use drawers, tabs, expandable panels, and dialogs inside those pages for secondary features. The speaking assistant is a persistent interface across the three pages, not a fourth page.

Fully automated means routine operation within an explicitly authorized account, instrument universe, capital allocation, operating mode, and risk budget. It does not mean inventing permissions or bypassing broker authentication. Default to paper mode. Once I deliberately enable live operation and its boundaries, ordinary qualifying trades should run without repeated approval.

I should never have to pretend that a connection, agent, trade, or result exists. Show clear states for disconnected, simulated, delayed, unavailable, and live.

ONE LAUNCH STARTS THE WHOLE SYSTEM

After initial installation and required account/provider setup, give me one obvious Start System launcher or button. Activating either must invoke the same startup workflow: start the backend, load persistent state, register all configured agents, connect available feeds and providers, reconcile accounts, run readiness checks, open the dashboard, and begin eligible automation in the saved authorized mode. Do not require a second Start Bot click or individual agent activation.

Start the supervisor and agent runtimes immediately when I launch the software. The brain automatically assigns work, selects eligible strategies, manages authorized trading, schedules its own evaluations, and recovers supported services. Starting or rebooting must never clear an emergency stop, loss-limit latch, or unresolved order incident. Keep a visible distinction between system running, agents available, data ready, and trading permitted. A running system can legitimately be waiting for its market to open.

3. MASTER AI AND SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE

ONE CENTRAL SUPERCOMPUTER BRAIN

Implement one shared intelligence and orchestration service called the Supercomputer Brain. It coordinates the trading fleet and is counted separately as a control service. Trading-agent identities initially run from A001 through A320; each manages its own market partition. Shared support services do not count toward the trading-agent target.

The brain owns market assignment, shared context, global operating constraints, and bounded capital allocation. Each trading agent selects eligible strategies and produces independent proposals for its own market partition. Give the reasoning agents one centrally configured model gateway, shared evidence store, and versioned market/portfolio state. I configure the supported AI connection once; I should not have to configure separate AI accounts or manually instruct each agent.

Trading agents receive bounded market assignments and run independent decision and position-management loops concurrently. The brain resolves assignment and aggregate-exposure conflicts, while deterministic risk and execution services enforce authorized limits on each proposal. Use one logical active coordinator with durable checkpoints and restart recovery; prevent two coordinators from becoming competing execution owners.

Show the Supercomputer Brain as the central service on page 2, connected to the scalable trading fleet, with desired and actual ready counts displayed separately; initial target 320. Show its actual model availability, workload, current plan, and shared-input state. The name describes coordinated software intelligence and does not imply additional physical computing hardware.

Separate the reasoning layer from the execution layer.

The master AI coordinates research and specialist outputs, explains decisions, selects among eligible strategies, and proposes portfolio actions. Deterministic services compute indicators, evaluate implemented rules, enforce risk policies, maintain order state, and communicate with brokers.

The language model must not directly hold trading credentials, invent prices, calculate authoritative balances, or bypass the risk engine. An unavailable model must not disable protective order management.

Use this operational flow:
market data -> quality checks -> features -> strategy signals -> portfolio allocation -> deterministic risk checks -> order management -> broker acknowledgements and fills -> reconciliation -> monitoring and explanation.

Research and learning run on a separate background path. New code, strategies, models, or parameters enter a versioned candidate registry and evaluation process before becoming eligible for execution.

Use typed, versioned messages. Every proposal should include an ID, timestamp, account and mode, instrument and venue, strategy version, data snapshot references, signal expiry, intended direction, proposed exposure, entry and exit conditions, estimated costs, risk impact, and a concise evidence-based explanation.

Support BUY, SELL, HOLD, EXIT, REDUCE, and ABSTAIN where meaningful. Abstaining is a successful outcome when evidence or execution conditions are inadequate.

Reject malformed, stale, unsupported, internally inconsistent, or unauthorized proposals. Store reason codes. Never interpret a model's self-reported confidence as a calibrated probability of profit.

Resolve conflicting signals centrally. Several agents must not independently submit contradictory orders for the same account and instrument. Aggregate exposures and dependencies across strategies and accounts. Correlated indicators or agents do not count as independent confirmation.

Prefer target-position proposals over repeated imperative "buy again" messages. Calculate the required change from reconciled holdings, outstanding orders, reserved exposure, and the approved target. Give each proposal a validity window and portfolio-state version; re-evaluate it if account state changes before execution.

Define three distinct memory stores:
- Operational state: authoritative orders, positions, policies, and connection state.
- Research knowledge: sources, hypotheses, specifications, versions, and evaluation artifacts.
- Conversational memory: user preferences and explanations, with retention controls.

A remembered conversation never overrides current broker state or an accepted risk policy. Use retrieval over versioned evidence rather than stuffing every agent transcript into the master model's context.

Every agent job needs a task contract: objective, input references, permitted tools, required output schema, dependencies, deadline, maximum attempts, cost allowance, cancellation state, and completion evidence. Workers return bounded results or explicit failure states. The coordinator must detect stalled dependencies and repeated handoffs instead of allowing endless loops.

SHARED REAL-TIME MARKET STATE FOR EVERY AGENT

Use a centrally managed ingestion service, an internal event bus, and a versioned market-state store. Normalize each provider update once and distribute it internally. Do not open duplicate external subscriptions for every agent when a shared subscription can supply the same entitled data.

Every configured agent must subscribe to common market-health, market-state-version, portfolio-state, and risk-policy updates. Deliver role-relevant price, depth, bar, event, funding, and volatility updates with the instrument scope and frequency that its task requires. Every agent can retrieve the same canonical snapshot; an unrelated worker need not send every raw tick to an LLM.

Attach provider event time, receipt time, venue, instrument, sequence/checkpoint, freshness, and snapshot version. Track the latest delivered and processed version for each agent. Reject decisions based on expired inputs or incompatible portfolio state. After a gap or reconnect, rebuild the required snapshot before resuming dependent work.

Publish measured feed delay, internal delivery lag, processing lag, and dropped or coalesced update counts. A connected socket or heartbeat is evidence of connection health, not proof that each subscribed instrument has a fresh quote. Preserve order/fill and risk events durably; only coalesce replaceable market updates when the strategy's data contract permits it.

Trading agents share typed evidence but make separate decisions for their assigned markets. Independent proposals can proceed concurrently through shared risk and order services. Aggregate portfolio accounting does not merge them into one trade. Use one logical order authority per account with concurrent instrument workflows, and retain each agent's ownership and attribution. Agent count and majority voting do not establish an edge.

CONTINUOUS GLOBAL STOCK AND CRYPTO MONITORING — ENTIRE TRADING FLEET

The trading fleet and its shared brain service must remain supervised and available around the clock while the execution host, network, power, and required services are available. They share one continuously updated view of the supported global stock and crypto universe. This does not require one external subscription per agent or repeated copies of every calculation. Every agent receives common health and state-version events, can query any covered market, and performs its role-specific work on assigned events or schedules. Idle and waiting-for-market states are healthy, observable states; a stale heartbeat is not.

Treat “all markets” as an explicit coverage objective with a measurable inventory, never a fabricated claim. Discover and maintain every instrument available through the connected, licensed providers, across enabled stock exchanges and crypto venues. Keep known-but-uncovered exchanges, assets, pairs, chains, and regions visible with a reason and acquisition requirement. Display separate counts for known, entitled, subscribed, fresh, delayed, unsupported, halted, and execution-eligible instruments. State the denominator: provider-discovered inventory is not proof of every listing on Earth.

Refresh instrument lists after provider notices and on a configurable daily schedule. Apply listing, delisting, symbol-change, split, and token-migration events atomically to the shared instrument master. Stock identities need exchange/MIC, share class, issuer, currency, and corporate-action history. Crypto identities need venue, spot/derivative type, base/quote, settlement asset, and chain/contract address where applicable. Never merge unrelated tokens just because they share a ticker, or confuse a tokenized equity with the underlying share.

Use licensed streaming quotes/trades for the broad subscribed universe, provider-supported snapshots where streaming is unavailable, and deeper order-book subscriptions where strategy requirements justify them. Disclose snapshot cadence and delay. A centralized scheduler prioritizes open positions, outstanding orders, and imminent risk before opportunity scans, then distributes broad-market scanning within a measured coverage deadline. Subscription limits or insufficient compute must produce a degraded coverage state with affected instruments, not silent omissions. Never buy an upgraded data plan automatically unless spending authority explicitly permits it.

Store the last event timestamp, receipt timestamp, current session, source, price type, and freshness threshold per instrument. Separate market closure, no recent trade, missing quote, broken subscription, delayed entitlement, and feed outage. A heartbeat proves only that a connection is alive. Normalize timestamps to UTC internally while showing exchange-local session time and the user's selected timezone. Keep primary/fallback-source provenance; a lower-quality fallback must not inherit the primary feed's status.

Stock-market monitoring continues through nights, weekends, holidays, auctions, and halts: process news, filings, corporate actions, eligible other-region sessions, portfolio exposure, and service health. Submit orders only in sessions and order types actually supported by the exchange and broker. Load current official calendars, early closes, daylight-saving changes, and product-specific restrictions; never hardcode that every stock trades 24/7. Crypto monitoring spans weekends and overnight, while respecting venue maintenance, chain interruptions, halts, and unavailable pairs. Reference mechanics: [S84, S85].

Use an always-on service supervisor with durable checkpoints, watchdogs, restart backoff, and resource budgets. Closing the dashboard must not kill the trading service. A powered-off or sleeping local computer cannot provide 24/7 processing: report that dependency and support deployment on an explicitly configured always-on host if uninterrupted service is required. Automatic host startup is a setup preference; it cannot clear an emergency stop or silently expand live authorization. A process restart must reconcile orders and restore subscriptions before new risk is permitted.

Page 2 must show the configured trading fleet, initially targeting 320 agents, and the separate central brain, with market coverage, assigned scan scope, latest processed state, heartbeat age, backlog, current task, and blocked dependencies. Its central market map must show stock sessions and crypto venue health, plus what the system cannot currently observe. Page 1 shows a concise monitoring summary; page 3 shows data entitlements, accounts, provider limits, and connection health. These additions do not create a fourth page.

Acceptance checks: one Start System action registers the configured trading fleet and reports actual readiness against target 320; a common market-state update is traceable to every role's receipt or checkpoint; all covered instruments have a freshness state; stock closure does not stop crypto or service monitoring; loss of one venue is visible and isolates affected strategies; reconnect rebuilds a consistent snapshot; no duplicate orders arise from failover; coverage and throughput claims have measured denominators. These are engineering checks run by the implementer, not user-facing backtest features.

MEME-COIN COVERAGE — EVERY CATEGORY, WITH MEASURED ACCESS

Explicitly include the full range of meme-coin categories in the crypto discovery and monitoring objective. Do not restrict coverage to a few famous tickers, a single blockchain, a single exchange, or a manually maintained watchlist. Continuously discover relevant coins and tokens from supported chain, venue, pool, and launchpad sources. The inventory is expandable; themes and examples are classification aids, not an exhaustive list of individual assets or a claim of universal access.

Classify assets on multiple independent axes so a token can belong to several categories:

| Coverage axis | Required scope | Implementation meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Theme | Animal, character, internet-culture, joke, parody, celebrity/personality, political, event-driven, community, AI-themed, gaming-themed, and emerging or unclassified memes | Store multiple versioned tags with source and confidence. A name, logo, theme, or claimed affiliation does not establish identity, endorsement, or trading quality. |
| Size and activity | Established liquid coins, medium and small caps, microcaps, newly launched tokens, dormant/revived assets, and inactive or failed projects | Keep circulating market cap separate from fully diluted value; mark unverifiable supply as unknown. Determine tradability from executable liquidity and data quality, not a price-times-supply headline alone. |
| Lifecycle | Minted but unlisted, initial launch, bonding-curve trading, pre-graduation, graduation/migration, AMM trading, CEX listing, relaunch, migration, delisting, and abandoned pools | Record actual lifecycle events and timestamps. A newly created asset with no market remains discoverable but has no executable quote. |
| Chain and asset form | Native-chain meme coins, standard fungible tokens, extension-enabled tokens, wrapped/bridged forms, and separately supported derivative instruments | Use native coin identity where no token contract exists. For tokens use chain plus contract/mint plus token program. Wrapped forms and perpetuals remain distinct instruments with linked underlying exposure. |
| Network coverage | Solana; Ethereum; supported EVM networks such as Base, BNB Chain, Arbitrum, Polygon, and Avalanche; and additional native or non-EVM networks as adapters become available | These are target network families, not a claim that integrations already exist. Discoverability, live data, transaction decoding, custody, and execution each need an explicit supported state. |
| Venue coverage | Centralized exchange listings, decentralized exchange pools, aggregators, and launchpad/bonding-curve venues | Track each venue/pool separately. Aggregated prices cannot replace an executable route with verified fees, size, and settlement. |
| Token behavior | Ordinary transfers, transfer-tax/reflection/rebasing designs, mint/freeze controls, custom hooks, upgradeable contracts, and unusual or unsupported behavior | Inventory broad categories while withholding execution when an adapter cannot correctly model transfer, ownership, and settlement behavior. All trading agents share the meme-coin universe through the canonical data service. Assign different supported chains, venues, pools, or instrument sets to separate trading workers. Shared data, liquidity, event, cost, security, and risk capabilities support their independent decisions. The brain coordinates allocation and exposure across concurrent trades. Include meme-coin workers within the scalable fleet target; no individual scanner-start action is required.

Run continuous discovery, price and liquidity monitoring, position supervision, and health checks after Start System, while the host and dependencies remain available. Listen for supported mint, pool-creation, trading-status, liquidity-change, and migration events; refresh provider listings and repair missed events after reconnect. Keep event time, observation time, chain block/slot, confirmation/finality status, and provider provenance. Separate tentative chain events from confirmed state and reverse affected derived state after a reorganization. Discovery speed is measured, not advertised as instantaneous knowledge of every new token.

Create a canonical record for each asset and a separate record for every tradable pair/pool. Include chain ID or native-chain identifier, contract/mint where applicable, token program, decimals, quote asset, venue/pool, creation time, lifecycle, supply confidence, issuer/control authorities, liquidity, holder-concentration confidence, current executable buy and sell routes, and supported operations. Treat ticker, name, social profile, and artwork as untrusted metadata. Duplicate names, counterfeit tokens, and bridged representations must never merge automatically. Distinguish pool vaults, custody addresses, and burn addresses from economically controlled holders when data permits; mark ambiguous ownership rather than inventing certainty.

Maintain separate states: discovered, monitored, data-incomplete, research-only, execution-eligible, suspended, and archived. Display current coverage denominators by chain and venue, last discovery time, stale feeds, unsupported token behavior, and the exact reason a coin cannot be traded. Unknown or newly emerging meme categories remain discoverable and can be classified later. No finite provider inventory proves coverage of every meme coin on the planet.

Strategy compatibility must be explicit. Trend, momentum, breakout, order-flow, relative-value, and on-chain-flow candidates may be researched for meme coins when their data and execution requirements hold. Existing examples include ST001–ST006, ST029–ST030, ST045–ST050, ST101–ST103, and ST108, each only where its actual instrument requirements apply. Stock financial-statement strategies do not acquire meme-coin applicability simply by changing the ticker. A token with no derivatives cannot use a futures-funding strategy; a brand-new token cannot supply a year of genuine observations. Insufficient history or missing markets must produce ABSTAIN or research-only status, never fabricated data. Category additions do not increase the strategy count or prove that any of the 124 templates is profitable on meme coins.

Before live eligibility, automatically evaluate executable liquidity at the intended order size; total fees, token taxes, gas and priority charges; slippage and price impact; route freshness; transfer restrictions; contract/program controls; concentration uncertainty; and the availability of an exit route. Apply the saved account's capital, per-token, per-venue, chain, and correlated-theme limits. Assess both entry and exit behavior using supported transaction simulation and current state. A successful simulation or external token score is evidence at one moment, not proof of future sellability or freedom from manipulation. Recheck after control, liquidity, route, or contract changes. Do not promote a token merely because its displayed volume, social engagement, or market cap is large.

Use a chain-specific execution adapter with exact integer units, bounded slippage, minimum received amount, transaction expiry, confirmation tracking, and reconciliation. Verify spending approvals and signing destinations against the intended route. Never treat a submitted transaction as a confirmed fill or blindly resubmit an ambiguous transaction. Handle pool migration without counting old and new pools as separate economic assets or issuing duplicate exposure. If a route disappears, preserve the real position and incident state; do not claim a completed sale or that a stop price guarantees an exit. Avoid automatic bridging or transfers into new custody arrangements outside existing user authorization. DEX trading-cost research provides context for these requirements [S90]; it is not evidence of a profitable meme-coin strategy.

Use public social and news information only from permitted sources, preserve publication timestamps, and separate measurable attention from promotional claims. Treat token descriptions, websites, social posts, and contract comments as untrusted input to every agent. They cannot issue instructions to the brain, change risk limits, request credentials, or authorize a trade. Bought engagement, repeated posts, wash activity, and uncertain attribution must lower confidence rather than create fictitious independent confirmation.

Place meme-coin discovery and exposure summaries inside page 1; add chain, lifecycle, liquidity, and coverage filters to page 2's existing agent/market view; place provider and wallet/exchange capabilities inside page 3. Show monitoring and execution status separately. Preserve exactly three pages, one Start System action, one central brain, and automatic agent evaluation without user-facing backtest controls.

Acceptance criteria: discover supported new tokens without a manually entered ticker; keep identically named assets on different chains distinct; monitor established coins and new launches concurrently; follow a supported launchpad migration without losing the position history; expose missing coverage; stop new entries when sellability or liquidity checks fail; reconcile uncertain chain transactions; and show all configured trading agents using the same versioned universe. These are software requirements, not claims that adapters or live trading have already been built.

4. AUTONOMOUS TRADING FLEET — TARGET 320 AGENTS, EXPANDABLE

This revision supersedes the fixed 50-role architecture. Build a default target of 320 autonomous trading agents, A001 through A320, each with its own market assignment and trading lifecycle. The shared Supercomputer Brain is a coordinating service outside the trading-agent count. Data ingestion, risk enforcement, execution gateways, voice, reconciliation, and other support services are also outside that count. Do not count support processes, empty tiles, copied prompts, or pending jobs as additional active traders.

Each trading agent must independently monitor its assigned instruments, select among eligible strategies, generate its own trade proposals, manage its assigned positions and exits, and maintain attributable results. The default interaction is concurrent activity across different market partitions. There is no requirement for all agents to vote on one opportunity or finish one shared trade before other markets can proceed. Sharing market facts and risk services does not merge all agents into a single trade decision.

ONE START, MANY CONCURRENT TRADING LOOPS

After setup, Start System starts the brain, supporting services, market connections, and the configured trading fleet without individual activation. Reconcile existing holdings and outstanding orders before allowing new entries. Maintain separate metrics for configured target, registered identities, running runtimes, data-ready agents, execution-eligible agents, current jobs, and agents with open positions. Report the true number against target 320; a pending or blocked identity is not a working trader. Completion requires actual functional agents and measured concurrent operation, with any remaining capacity or access shortfall stated explicitly.

An agent is a persistent logical trading worker with a stable ID, market partition, capability profile, data subscriptions, strategy versions, virtual capital budget, outstanding-order ownership, position ledger, session deadline, heartbeat, and recovery checkpoint. Workers may use deterministic rules, statistical models, and bounded model calls. They need independent task queues and fault isolation; a slow agent must not block unrelated markets. Batching model requests and sharing computed features is allowed, but serializing the entire fleet behind a single market decision is not.

INITIAL COVERAGE TARGETS — DYNAMIC ASSIGNMENTS

| Trading-agent IDs | Initial coverage group | Target workers | Partition examples |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| A001–A096 | Global stocks, ETFs, and listed equity exposures | 96 | Region, exchange session, sector, eligible instrument set, and liquidity tier |
| A097–A224 | Crypto, including every supported meme-coin category | 128 | Chain, venue, spot/derivative type, pool set, lifecycle, and verified instrument set |
| A225–A256 | Foreign exchange | 32 | Eligible currency-pair groups, venue, and session |
| A257–A288 | Commodity and index futures | 32 | Exchange, contract family, maturity group, and liquidity window |
| A289–A304 | Listed options and volatility products | 16 | Underlying groups, approved structures, and expiry/settlement constraints |
| A305–A312 | Rates, bonds, and credit-related instruments | 8 | Executable products and hedges with adequate intraday liquidity |
| A313–A320 | Supported specialty markets and overflow coverage | 8 | Verified power, emissions, weather, other supported products, or reassigned eligible market partitions |
| Total | Initial target | 320 | Counts allocate work, not capital or promised returns |

These are startup routing targets, not permanent assignments or a requirement to trade illiquid products. The brain updates partitions as sessions open, listings change, data becomes available, and queues build. Every worker retains a stable ID while assignment history records its actual scope. If a group lacks access or useful work, retain its coverage gap and reassign available workers to distinct eligible partitions elsewhere. An instrument universe may contain far more than 320 markets; each worker can scan a bounded collection. Do not pretend there are 320 unrelated asset classes or allocate one worker per token indefinitely.

ALL MARKETS AS A COVERAGE OBJECTIVE

Discover and schedule the full instrument inventory available through the authorized providers across all supported asset classes, including the meme-coin taxonomy. Track every known uncovered region, exchange, chain, product, and instrument with a reason. “All markets at the same time” means concurrent coverage and independent trading across every accessible eligible market partition, subject to actual market sessions, data freshness, and execution access. Closed, halted, unsupported, private, or unquoted markets cannot be represented as continuously tradable. Show known, covered, current, and eligible counts with explicit denominators.

DAY-TRADING OPERATING MODE

Make intraday trading the active operating mode. Every live candidate needs a declared entry window, maximum holding duration, end-of-session entry cutoff, and flattening deadline. For session-based markets, target closure before the configured executable session ends. For continuously traded crypto, use a documented rolling maximum hold and daily risk cutoff with an explicit timezone. Stop opening positions too close to the cutoff. Continue monitoring around the clock, while submitting orders only when the relevant market and account permit them.

The 124-card catalog and 212-entry signal index include longer-horizon methods. Keep their source horizons and evidence intact. Monthly factors, staking allocations, long-lived corporate-event trades, and other incompatible methods remain research-only in intraday mode unless a separately specified intraday version passes internal evaluation. Changing a holding period does not inherit the original method's empirical support. Agents perform this evaluation automatically; no user-facing backtest or manual-training control is introduced.

Manage only system-owned positions. A halt, failed route, or unavailable liquidity may prevent timely closure: retain the position and risk incident, stop affected new entries, and continue authorized exit management when execution becomes possible. Do not fabricate a flat position or liquidate unrelated user holdings. Exposure and completed fills come from reconciliation, not the agent's intended state.

MARKET OWNERSHIP, CAPITAL, AND CONCURRENT EXECUTION

Give each account/instrument exposure one logical owning trading agent at a time by default. Different agents can trade different instruments and market partitions concurrently. If a multi-leg strategy spans partitions, reserve all required legs for the strategy owner before execution; do not let another worker independently cancel or reverse its hedge. Use ownership leases with versioned transfer, durable checkpoints, and stale-owner rejection. On reassignment, transfer or finish existing positions and orders explicitly before the new owner assumes control.

Virtual agent budgets must sum within the authorized account limits; 320 agents do not each receive permission to spend the full account balance. The deterministic risk service atomically reserves capital and exposure across simultaneous proposals, considers pending orders and correlated positions, and rejects excess or conflicting requests. Trade allocation is per agent and strategy; portfolio aggregation is for total risk and accounting, not a requirement to reduce all opportunities to one trade.

The shared order-management service supports concurrent outstanding orders and independently managed positions across instruments. Use per-account/instrument ordering only where correctness requires it, plus provider-wide rate limits and priority for protective actions. Preserve one logical order authority per account to prevent duplicate submission during failover; this authority may process many instruments concurrently. Every order, fill, cost, and position allocation carries trading-agent ID, strategy version, assignment version, and account mode. Attribute broker-netted executions through an explicit allocation ledger.

The brain allocates market scopes and bounded capital, supervises health and aggregate exposure, and resolves ownership conflicts. It need not make an LLM call or collect every worker's approval for every ordinary qualifying trade. Each eligible strategy loop continues automatically within its existing authorization. Failed optional reasoning must not disable protective position management or invent missing signals.

SCALING BEYOND 300 WITHOUT FICTITIOUS CAPACITY

Set desired_agents=320 as the initial target, with configurable expansion beyond 320 within provisioned host capacity and approved data/model/compute budgets. Do not impose an architectural ceiling at 50 or 300. Persist the desired count and actual ready count separately. Use measured queue age, scan completion, missed deadlines, API throughput, memory, CPU/GPU use, model latency, and cost to determine how many workers can perform useful independent work.

Increase the fleet only when additional distinct eligible partitions or backlog justify it and resources can sustain the required data and decision deadlines. Use bounded scaling steps and cooldowns. If capacity is insufficient, preserve protective services, reduce optional research load, and report the exact shortfall; never show 320 green agents merely because 320 records exist. Reducing fleet size requires draining or transferring orders and positions first. Never abandon an owned position, silently broaden account permissions, or purchase infrastructure/data subscriptions beyond authorized spending.

Keep workers supervised 24/7 while the host and dependencies remain available. Use restart recovery, bounded retries, checkpoint restoration, and fault isolation. A locally sleeping or powered-off host cannot provide uninterrupted service. Supported always-on deployment must be configured explicitly. Three hundred twenty logical workers do not require 320 simultaneous language-model generations or 320 independent paid accounts.

PAGE 2 AND COMPLETION EVIDENCE

On AI Supercomputer — Agent Activity, show a shared brain plus a virtualized, searchable fleet view: A001–A320 initially, automatically extending when configured. Each card shows actual assigned markets, strategy, state, most recent data and decision, positions, outstanding orders, allocated budget, realized/unrealized results, costs, next deadline, and failure reasons. Group by asset class, region, venue, chain, and session. Display support-service health separately so support components do not inflate the trading-agent count. Preserve the three-page structure and one Start System action.

Demonstrate multiple agents processing separate market updates, independently generating distinct proposals, managing concurrently outstanding orders on different instruments, and exiting their own positions without waiting for an unrelated trade. At the configured target, measure scan coverage and decision deadlines under representative load. Verify atomic capital reservations, unique instrument ownership, reconnect recovery, failed-worker isolation, and position-safe reassignment. Report actual outcomes rather than claiming concurrency from a grid animation. All available agents may run simultaneously; that does not require every agent to find a trade or hold a position continuously.

SHARED OPERATIONAL CAPABILITIES

Retain the following functional coverage as services or bounded shared jobs. These capability IDs are not trading-agent IDs and are not counted in the target of 320. No single capability is a substitute for an autonomous trading worker.

C01. Supercomputer Brain / fleet coordinator: assigns separate market partitions and capital budgets, supervises independent trading workers, and publishes shared operating constraints.
C02. Market-data supervisor: manages feeds, subscriptions, timestamps, and availability.
C03. Data-quality analyst: detects gaps, stale quotes, duplicate events, and inconsistent instruments.
C04. Regime analyst: identifies trend, range, volatility, liquidity, and uncertain conditions.
C05. Trend specialist: evaluates eligible trend-following systems.
C06. Momentum specialist: evaluates momentum and relative-strength signals.
C07. Mean-reversion specialist: evaluates range and reversion candidates.
C08. Breakout specialist: evaluates range breaks and volatility expansion.
C09. Market-structure specialist: evaluates explicitly defined swing and structure rules.
C10. Volume and order-flow specialist: uses only genuinely available volume and depth data.
C11. Statistical-arbitrage specialist: evaluates pairs and spread relationships.
C12. Crypto specialist: handles venue structure, funding inputs, and eligible spot instruments.
C13. Equity specialist: handles corporate actions, sessions, and equity-specific constraints.
C14. FX specialist: handles currency pairs, sessions, rollover, and conversion.
C15. Futures and index-products specialist: handles contracts, expiry, multipliers, and eligible index-linked products.
C16. News and event analyst: tracks timestamped, attributable events.
C17. Strategy researcher: maintains a deduplicated, sourced catalog of candidates.
C18. Validation analyst: evaluates candidates automatically and documents uncertainty.
C19. Portfolio allocator: proposes exposure within approved constraints.
C20. Risk-policy analyst: reviews risk and explains deterministic policy outcomes.
C21. Execution analyst: selects supported execution approaches and monitors execution quality.
C22. Reconciliation analyst: checks orders, fills, balances, and positions against broker records.
C23. Attribution analyst: measures outcomes by strategy, market, costs, and operating regime.
C24. Reliability supervisor: monitors failures, resource use, recoveries, and drift.
C25. Voice and reporting assistant: explains verified system state and handles authorized commands.
C26. Liquidity analyst: measures available depth, executable size, and capacity for proposed orders.
C27. Spread monitor: tracks bid/ask spreads and rejects unsuitable transaction conditions.
C28. Transaction-cost estimator: estimates fees, slippage, impact, and execution shortfall.
C29. Borrowing analyst: checks short availability, borrow costs, recalls, and applicable restrictions.
C30. Funding analyst: tracks financing, rollover, collateral costs, and eligible funding payments.
C31. Event-calendar supervisor: maintains timestamped economic, earnings, expiry, and scheduled event calendars.
C32. Corporate-actions analyst: handles splits, dividends, mergers, symbol changes, and delistings.
C33. Session-calendar supervisor: enforces instrument sessions, holidays, auctions, and daylight-saving changes.
C34. Contract-roll analyst: tracks futures expiry, delivery avoidance, contract selection, and rolls.
C35. Correlation analyst: measures shared strategy and asset exposures and changing dependencies.
C36. Concentration analyst: monitors instrument, issuer, sector, currency, and venue concentrations.
C37. Stress-scenario analyst: evaluates portfolio behavior under defined shocks and liquidity disruptions.
C38. Strategy-deduplication analyst: identifies equivalent candidates, shared rules, and duplicated signals.
C39. Parameter-stability analyst: examines parameter sensitivity and flags fragile candidate behavior.
C40. Signal-calibration analyst: checks whether probabilistic outputs match observed outcomes and reports uncertainty.
C41. Model-drift monitor: detects deterioration in model outputs and their measured usefulness.
C42. Data-drift monitor: detects changes in feature distributions, schemas, and data coverage.
C43. Reconciliation-anomaly investigator: diagnoses mismatches flagged by the reconciliation service.
C44. Security-event monitor: tracks authorization failures, suspicious access, and secret-handling incidents.
C45. Compute-and-API budget supervisor: schedules resource use and enforces approved compute and provider budgets.
C46. Storage-health supervisor: monitors database integrity, disk capacity, retention, and backup health.
C47. Restart-recovery coordinator: restores checkpoints and verifies ownership and reconciliation before resumption.
C48. Voice-intent validator: resolves spoken commands into authenticated, scoped application actions.
C49. Alert-priority coordinator: deduplicates, prioritizes, expires, and routes operational alerts.
C50. Session-reporting analyst: produces factual summaries of trades, costs, incidents, and remaining exposure.

5. RESEARCH AND STRATEGY LIBRARY

Build a continuously expanding strategy research library. Do not claim to have learned every strategy in existence, discovered private proprietary methods, or established that any method always works.

For each strategy, distinguish:
- Documented idea.
- Precisely specified candidate.
- Implemented candidate.
- Automatically evaluated candidate.
- Paper-eligible strategy.
- Live-eligible strategy within an authorized scope.
- Suspended or retired strategy.

Research these families and their meaningful variations:

Trend: moving-average rules, channel breakouts, Donchian systems, trend pullbacks, volatility-adjusted trends, and time-series momentum.

Momentum: rate of change, cross-sectional strength, relative strength, session momentum, and momentum with liquidity or volatility filters.

Mean reversion: standardized price deviations, Bollinger-type bands, moving-average distance, range reversion, and suitable VWAP deviations.

Breakouts: opening ranges, prior-session levels, consolidation ranges, squeeze expansion, failed breakouts, and objectively defined retests.

Structure: confirmed swings, support and resistance, break-of-structure rules, liquidity-sweep hypotheses, and explicit imbalance definitions. Treat smart-money and similar terminology as hypotheses needing measurable rules.

Volume and order flow: relative volume, trade imbalance, order-book imbalance, volume profiles, and spread-depth interactions. Disable these when the data cannot support them.

Relative value: pairs, cointegration candidates, basket residuals, sector-relative signals, and mean-reverting spreads, with relationship-breakdown controls.

Crypto-specific: eligible spot momentum, basis and funding research, cross-venue discrepancies, and liquidity filters. Research status must not imply that derivatives or cross-venue execution are available.

FX: trend, range, session transitions, macro-event filters, relative currency strength, and rollover-aware execution.

Futures and index-linked instruments: eligible contract momentum, opening ranges, calendar-spread research, expiry-aware signals, and contract-roll handling. Indices themselves are benchmarks; execution requires an eligible tradable product.

Event-driven: earnings and scheduled macro-event reactions where licensed, timestamped data supports the hypothesis.

Statistical and machine-learning methods: calibrated classification, regression, regime models, change detection, and carefully evaluated ensembles.

Portfolio methods: volatility scaling, diversification, correlation-aware allocation, and bounded regime-dependent weighting.

Execution methods: limit-order scheduling, participation controls, TWAP/VWAP-style execution where justified, and implementation-shortfall analysis. Label these as execution methods, not automatic sources of profit.

For every catalog entry, store the source, publication date, originally studied markets and horizons, precise rules, inputs, parameter ranges, costs, failure modes, compute requirements, market eligibility, version, and current evidence status.

A long-horizon study does not establish an edge on five-minute candles. A working implementation does not establish profitability. A variation of the same formula does not become an independent strategy merely by changing its name.

Use primary research, exchange and broker documentation, and reproducible sources. Record uncertainty and contradictory findings. Never label an internet win-rate claim as verified evidence.

Distribute the supported market inventory across concurrent workers, prioritizing open positions and freshness deadlines. Support intraday strategies only where evidence, executable liquidity, and data justify them. Preserve source horizons in the research catalog; longer-horizon methods require a separately evaluated intraday adaptation before use in day-trading mode. Cross-market support must include correct instrument semantics, not one generic stock formula reused everywhere.

Assign each candidate a stable family ID and rule fingerprint so cosmetic names and small parameter changes do not inflate the strategy count. Record rejected candidates and failed hypotheses to prevent the research agent from repeatedly rediscovering them.

Schedule research in bounded batches. Stop each batch with an evidence register, newly specified candidates, contradictions, and a concrete next action. A request to research broadly must never become an infinite loop that blocks delivery of the working application.

Treat options, on-chain trading, market making, high-frequency execution, and cross-venue arbitrage as capability-gated extensions. Enable an extension only after its distinct data, permissions, execution, and risk requirements are implemented. For on-chain work, a token ticker alone is insufficient identification; chain and contract identity are required.

5A. WHAT "BEST" AND "WORKING" MUST MEAN

Interpret my request as broad coverage of credible systematic methods across accessible public markets, followed by automatic selection for the actual account and current conditions. There is no single best strategy across all assets, horizons, costs, and regimes. Build an expanding evidence catalog; do not represent this initial catalog as every public or proprietary method in existence.

A strategy is "working software" when its implementation behaves according to its specification. It is "currently eligible" only when its data, validation, permissions, execution, and risk conditions pass. Historical research support is a separate label. None of these states guarantees future profits.

Use three evidence labels throughout the catalog:
- R — Research-supported family: the cited authors report empirical evidence in a specified historical sample. This does not certify the proposed implementation, current profitability, or transfer to another market.
- C — Documented construction: the trade, hedge, or portfolio structure is documented by a primary source. Its mechanics do not establish a positive expected net return.
- H — Research hypothesis or adaptation: the concrete proposed rule has not been established by the sources reviewed for this prompt. Keep it in research until the system produces appropriate evidence.

Record source access depth separately: full methods inspected, abstract inspected, or bibliographic record only. If the full method is unavailable, do not pretend the paper has been replicated. R describes what the published study reports; implementation readiness remains unverified.

Every specification below is a starting template. Holding periods are research scopes, not optimized recommendations. Proposed entry, exit, sizing, and suspension rules are engineering requirements unless explicitly identified as an exact source method. A change in timing, asset universe, hedge, parameters, or execution creates a new version requiring its own evaluation.

This catalog contains 124 templates, including related variants and defensive structures. The count does not represent 124 independent return sources. Identify shared trend, equity, credit, volatility, liquidity, and funding exposures before combining them. Covered calls, collars, and protective puts may serve portfolio objectives without being standalone alpha strategies.

5B. MARKET COVERAGE AND ADAPTER REQUIREMENTS

Support a market only through verified instruments, data, account permissions, and execution adapters. Make a coverage matrix with separate research, historical-data, live-data, paper-execution, and live-execution status.

| Market | Research scope | Additional implementation requirements |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Global equities | Eligible shares across regions, sectors, and capitalization groups | Point-in-time listings, delistings, dividends, splits, filing timestamps, currencies, trading halts, short availability, and local settlement rules. |
| ETFs, listed REITs, and index products | Equity, sector, country, bond, commodity, and eligible crypto exposures | Product mandate, holdings timing, distributions, tracking differences, premium/discount, leverage/reset mechanics, and underlying trading hours. An index value is not an executable instrument. |
| FX | Eligible spot, forwards, and currency futures | Base/quote conventions, executable bid/ask, actual financing, settlement dates, holidays, dealer-specific volume, and instrument-specific rollover. Do not portray one dealer's volume as global FX volume. |
| Crypto | Eligible spot, dated futures, and perpetual contracts, including meme coins across the explicit coverage categories | Venue and chain identity, custody and counterparty exposure, contract collateral, funding timing, outages, delistings, and derivative eligibility. Stablecoin collateral is not assumed equivalent to insured cash. |
| Commodities | Energy, metals, agriculture, livestock, and supported specialty contracts | Delivery specifications, expiry, first-notice dates, units, conversion ratios, seasonality, storage information, inventory release timestamps, and real contract rolls. |
| Rates and sovereign bonds | Cash bonds, rate futures, yield-curve and basis research | Accrued interest, clean/dirty prices, day counts, coupon schedules, duration, DV01, convexity, cheapest-to-deliver dynamics, financing, and settlement. |
| Corporate and emerging-market credit | Eligible bonds, bond funds, and explicitly supported hedging instruments | Tradable quotes, issue-level liquidity, default/recovery assumptions, issuer concentration, spread duration, callability, ratings history, and stale-price detection. |
| Listed options | Eligible equity, ETF, index, and futures options | Full contract identity, multiplier, exercise style, settlement, executable chains, Greeks with model versions, dividends, assignment, expiration, and combination-order support. |
| Specialty markets | Power, emissions, freight, volatility products, and other requested instruments | Research-only until a dedicated product specification, licensed data, account access, and risk model exist. Do not infer support from a similarly named ticker. |

Direct property, private credit, private equity, and other infrequently traded assets need separate workflows; do not present them as continuously executable markets. Prediction markets and other specialized venues require a separate access and product review before inclusion.

5C. EXPANDING CATALOG: 124 STRATEGY TEMPLATES

A. CROSS-ASSET TREND, VALUE, AND CARRY

ST001 — Time-series momentum. Evidence R [S01]. Markets: liquid futures and forwards in the studied asset classes; scope: weeks to months. Use each instrument's trailing excess return to determine direction, with bounded volatility scaling. Exit or rebalance when the scheduled signal changes. Require appropriate return and roll series. Main failures: reversals, whipsaws, gaps, and correlated trend exposure.

ST002 — Moving-average trend. Evidence H for the proposed variant. Markets: liquid shares, ETFs, FX, crypto, and futures; scope: days to months. Define a completed-bar price/average or fast/slow crossover, a buffer, and a scheduled exit. Include spreads and turnover. Test each market separately; choppy prices and delayed reversals can repeatedly erode capital.

ST003 — Donchian/channel breakout. Evidence H for the proposed variant. Markets: liquid spot and futures products; scope: days to months. Enter beyond a channel calculated from prior completed bars, excluding the trigger bar. Exit through a separately defined trailing channel or risk rule. Handle gaps and stop slippage; false breaks and correlated signals can dominate results.

ST004 — Relative momentum with an absolute-trend filter. Evidence H for this combination. Markets: diversified eligible ETFs or futures; scope: monthly decisions. Rank assets by trailing strength, then allocate only when the selected asset passes an absolute threshold. Cash is a valid target. Define ties, turnover limits, and exposure caps; rapid rotation and concentration are failure modes.

ST005 — Volatility-managed momentum. Evidence R for volatility management at the studied portfolio level, H for this combination [S03]. Markets: an already specified momentum portfolio. Scale exposure from lagged volatility within fixed caps; rebalance on a defined schedule. Test estimation lag and transaction costs. Low measured volatility must never authorize unlimited leverage or conceal jump risk.

ST006 — Trend pullback continuation. Evidence H. Markets: liquid instruments with a separately defined trend; scope: intraday to several days. Define the trend, allowable retracement, completed-bar trigger, and invalidation before evaluating it. Exit at invalidation, target, or maximum age. Suspended during unclear structure; a reversal cannot be relabeled a pullback after losses.

ST007 — Cross-asset value. Evidence R [S02]. Markets: the asset groups and valuation measures supported by the selected study; scope: months to years. Use asset-specific valuation, normalize within comparable groups, and rebalance gradually. Require point-in-time economic or fundamental data. Cheap assets can become cheaper; macro breaks, financing, and concentration can overwhelm the valuation signal.

ST008 — Diversified carry. Evidence R [S04]. Markets: supported currencies, bonds, commodities, and other separately specified instruments; scope: weeks to months. Estimate carry under explicitly unchanged price/curve conditions, rank comparable exposures, and diversify. Include financing and hedging. Carry is conditional compensation, not locked profit; correlated losses can emerge during funding and liquidity stress.

B. EQUITIES, SECTORS, AND CORPORATE EVENTS

ST009 — Cross-sectional equity momentum. Evidence R [S02, S05]. Markets: liquid equities; scope: monthly formation and multi-month holding variants. Rank lagged total returns using a predefined observation and skip period; buy relative winners and short losers only if permitted. Account for delistings, borrow, and turnover. Momentum can suffer sharp losses around market rebounds.

ST010 — Sector or industry rotation. Evidence H for the proposed implementation. Markets: eligible sector baskets or ETFs; scope: weeks to months. Rank comparable total-return series and rebalance among stronger sectors with concentration caps. Exit when rank or trend conditions fail. Overlapping constituents, changing classifications, index reconstruction, and crowded sector exposure can create misleading diversification.

ST011 — Book-to-market value. Evidence R [S06]. Markets: equities with suitable accounting data; scope: months to years. Rank a precisely defined point-in-time book-to-market measure within the chosen universe and rebalance on schedule. Require publication lags and valid denominators. Financial distress, intangible-heavy businesses, sector bias, and long periods of underperformance require explicit controls.

ST012 — Earnings or free-cash-flow yield value. Evidence H for the chosen definitions and composite. Markets: equities; scope: months to years. Compare timestamped earnings or cash flow with the appropriate price or enterprise-value denominator. Define treatment of negative values and one-off items. Exit on scheduled reranking or invalid data; accounting distortions and value traps can defeat the signal.

ST013 — Quality minus junk. Evidence R [S07]. Markets: equities; scope: months. Build an explicitly versioned quality score using source-supported dimensions, then form a diversified portfolio. Use only data published before selection. Rebalance rather than chase news narratives. Valuation, sector crowding, missing fields, and financing can make a high-quality company an unattractive trade.

ST014 — Operating-profitability factor. Evidence R [S06]. Markets: equities; scope: months. Follow a documented accounting definition and rank stronger versus weaker operating profitability within an eligible universe. Keep reporting delays and rebalance rules explicit. Profitability is distinct from recent price momentum, but concentrated sector exposure and revised accounts can still bias an apparent result.

ST015 — Conservative-investment factor. Evidence R [S06]. Markets: equities; scope: months to years. Define asset-growth or investment intensity from point-in-time accounts and form conservative-versus-aggressive groups. Follow a documented rebalance schedule. Acquisitions, accounting changes, small-company liquidity, and sustained growth-led markets can undermine performance or comparability.

ST016 — Betting against beta. Evidence R [S08]. Markets: instruments supported by the chosen study; scope: monthly portfolio decisions. Estimate beta using lagged data and specify the scaling of low- and high-beta legs. Rebalance with financing and gross-exposure limits. If leverage or shorts are unavailable, treat a long-only adaptation as a different strategy.

ST017 — Long-only low-volatility portfolio. Evidence H for the proposed adaptation; related beta research [S08]. Markets: liquid equities or ETFs; scope: months. Rank lagged risk estimates and construct a constrained portfolio. Define benchmark exposure and rebalance costs. This is not equivalent to betting against beta; concentration, valuation, and rising correlation can offset lower standalone volatility.

ST018 — Short-term reversal. Evidence C for the documented factor construction, H for the executable rule [S09]. Markets: liquid equities; scope: days to weeks. Buy recent relative losers and, where permitted, sell winners using a fixed formation horizon. Enforce spread and news exclusions. Turnover, adverse selection, and distressed-name concentration can consume the apparent gross effect.

ST019 — Post-earnings-announcement drift. Evidence H pending full-method extraction for this implementation; foundational literature located [S10]. Markets: equities; scope: days to months after publication. Define a timestamped earnings-surprise measure and trade only after dissemination. Specify exit age and next-event handling. Revised estimates, instant repricing, borrow costs, and publication-time leakage can invalidate results.

ST020 — Merger arbitrage. Evidence R [S11]. Markets: announced, eligible corporate transactions; scope: weeks to deal resolution. Calculate consideration and deal-specific hedges from verified public terms, then compare spread with an independently specified risk model. Exit on completion, thesis invalidation, or constraints. Deal breaks, competing bids, funding, and uncertain completion times create asymmetric losses.

C. INTRADAY AND MARKET-MICROSTRUCTURE CANDIDATES

ST021 — Opening-range breakout. Evidence R for the cited crude-oil study's specific threshold method, H for generic first-N-minute variants [S12]. Markets: separately evaluated liquid session-based instruments; scope: one session. Freeze an opening range or source-specific threshold, enter on a later executable break, and exit by a defined deadline. Include failed breaks, gaps, and spread expansion.

ST022 — First-half-hour to last-half-hour momentum. Evidence R [S13]. Initial scope: the S&P 500 ETF setting studied by the authors. The opening signal includes the return from the previous close through the first half hour; evaluate a separately defined final-half-hour trade. Do not reinterpret this evidence as prediction of every subsequent five-minute bar.

ST023 — VWAP trend pullback. Evidence H. Markets: instruments with reliable trade-volume data; scope: intraday. In a separately confirmed trend, identify a pullback toward session VWAP and enter only after a predefined resumption trigger. Exit on invalidation or session deadline. VWAP is a reference price, not evidence that price must bounce.

ST024 — VWAP mean reversion. Evidence H. Markets: liquid, reliably measured session instruments; scope: minutes to hours. Fade a standardized deviation only when the specified range regime and liquidity checks pass. Target the moving reference with a time limit. Trend days, scheduled information releases, fragmented volume, and widening spreads can defeat reversion.

ST025 — Prior-session high/low breakout. Evidence H. Markets: session-based equities, FX feeds, and futures; scope: intraday. Fix calendar, prior-session bounds, breakout buffer, and permitted entry times. Exit on failure or deadline. Distinguish overnight from regular-session levels; repeated touches, auction effects, and transaction costs can eliminate a visible chart pattern's value.

ST026 — Failed-breakout reversal. Evidence H. Markets: liquid instruments; scope: intraday to several days. Define a previously known range, a measurable excursion, and a later completed-bar return inside it. Enter only after confirmation, with invalidation outside the excursion. Do not use future bars to select failures; genuine trend continuation is the principal risk.

ST027 — Gap continuation. Evidence H. Markets: session-based equities and futures; scope: intraday. Classify the opening gap using only prior-close and available opening information, then require a defined continuation trigger. Exclude corporate-action artifacts and uncertain event data. Exit on invalidation or time. Opening volatility, auctions, and rapid reversal can dominate results.

ST028 — Gap fade. Evidence H. Markets: separately eligible session instruments; scope: intraday. Require an objective gap size, a non-event or other explicitly justified eligibility condition, and a reversal trigger. Target a predeclared portion of the gap with bounded loss and duration. A gap is not an obligation for the market to return to yesterday's price.

ST029 — Volatility-squeeze expansion. Evidence H. Markets: liquid instruments; scope: minutes to days. Define compression from lagged realized volatility or band width, freeze the breakout level, and trade a subsequent move. Specify false-break and time exits. Do not count Bollinger, ATR, and channel variants as independent evidence; spread and volatility can expand together.

ST030 — Order-book imbalance response. Evidence R for a studied short-horizon predictive relationship, H for a profitable trading rule [S14]. Markets: venues with complete, synchronized depth. Estimate imbalance and evaluate executable responses at measured latency. Exit after a short signal lifetime. Queue position, cancellations, hidden liquidity, fees, and adverse selection are essential.

ST031 — Confirmed market-structure break. Evidence H. Markets: liquid instruments; scope: minutes to days. Define swing confirmation using a fixed number of already completed bars and timestamp when the level becomes known. Trade a later break with explicit invalidation. Repainting pivots or hindsight-selected structure disqualify the implementation.

ST032 — Liquidity sweep and reclaim. Evidence H. Markets: supported liquid instruments; scope: intraday. Define a known level, minimum excursion, reclaim timing, and entry condition. Call inferred stop concentrations a hypothesis unless actual data supports them. Exit on renewed breach or timeout. Do not claim access to other traders' private stops or hidden intentions.

D. STATISTICAL ARBITRAGE AND RELATIVE VALUE

ST033 — Distance-based pairs trading. Evidence R [S15]. Markets: suitable equity pairs; scope: days to months. Select pairs using a past formation window and normalized historical-price distance, then define divergence and convergence rules for the next trading interval. Include both legs' costs and borrow. Structural breaks, common factor exposure, and delistings remain material.

ST034 — Cointegration-based pairs. Evidence H for the exact selection and execution model. Markets: economically plausible tradable pairs; scope: days to months. Estimate a past-window hedge and residual, require relationship diagnostics, and trade bounded deviations. Exit on convergence, timeout, or relationship failure.
